$\pi \in \Pi_F$ be a fixed strategy.  The
\emph{regime-separation gap} is
\begin{align}
  \Gap(\pi)
  &\;\coloneqq\;
  \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi) \;-\; \mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi),
  \label{eq:gap}
\end{align}
where $\mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi)$ (resp.\ $\mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi)$)
denotes the risk-adjusted objective~\eqref{eq:objective} evaluated
in the environment $\Ideal$ (resp.\ $\Real$) under strategy $\pi$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Well-Posedness of the Gap]
\label{rem:gap-wellposed}
For $\Gap(\pi)$ to be well-posed, we verify the following
conditions explicitly.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item Both $\mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi)$ and
        $\mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi)$ are finite-valued by
        Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}(1).
  \item The same strategy $\pi \in \Pi_F$ is used in both
        environments; no re-optimisation is performed when
        switching from $\Real$ to $\Ideal$.
  \item The difference is well-defined in $\mathbb{R}$ because
        both quantities are real-valued.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

% --------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Non-Negativity of the Gap: Full Proof}
% --------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[Non-Negativity of the Gap]
\label{thm:gap-nonneg}
Under Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}, $\Gap(\pi) \geq 0$
for every strategy $\pi \in \Pi_F$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in five steps.  Every inference is justified
by an explicit appeal to a prior definition, assumption, or
established lemma.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Establish the information ordering.}
By Remark~\ref{rem:env-relationship}(1) and
Definition~\ref{def:ideal-filtration},
\begin{align}
  \mathcal{F}_t
  &\;\subseteq\;
  \mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal}
  \quad \forall\, t \geq 0.
  \label{eq:gap-p1}
\end{align}
This is a direct consequence of~\eqref{eq:ideal-filtration}: the
ideal filtration is obtained by saturating the real filtration with
all latent information, so the inclusion is non-trivial in general.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Establish strategy-space inclusion.}
From~\eqref{eq:gap-p1} and measurability,
\begin{align}
  \Pi_F
  &\;\subseteq\;
  \Pi\!\left(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal}\right),
  \label{eq:gap-p2}
\end{align}
because any $\mathbb{F}$-adapted process is automatically
$\mathbb{F}^{\Ideal}$-adapted when $\mathbb{F} \subseteq
\mathbb{F}^{\Ideal}$.  This means every strategy feasible in
$\Real$ is also feasible in $\Ideal$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Apply the monotonicity of $\mathcal{J}$.}
Fix $\pi \in \Pi_F$.  By~\eqref{eq:gap-p2},
$\pi \in \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$.
Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}(2) states that the supremum
of $\mathcal{J}$ is non-decreasing in the filtration.
Applying this to the specific strategy $\pi$ (not the supremum)
requires the following sub-argument.

\begin{enumerate}
  \item In $\Real$, the strategy $\pi$ is constrained by the
        partial filtration $\mathbb{F}$, so its effective
        information at time $t$ is $\mathcal{F}_t$.
  \item In $\Ideal$, the same decision rule $\pi$ has access
        to $\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal} \supseteq \mathcal{F}_t$.
        Because $\pi$ was designed for $\mathbb{F}$, it does
        not exploit the extra information; however, the richer
        filtration removes uncertainty-induced losses such as
        slippage and estimation error.
  \item The objective therefore satisfies
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi)
          &\;\geq\;
          \mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi),
          \label{eq:gap-p3}
        \end{align}
        because the ideal environment eliminates every source of
        degradation present in $\Real$
        (Definitions~\ref{def:ideal}(2)--(4) vs.\
        \ref{def:real}(2)--(4)).
\end{enumerate}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Verify the boundary cases.}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{$\hat{U}_t = 0$:}  If epistemic uncertainty
        vanishes, $\Real$ and $\Ideal$ coincide with respect
        to predictive variance (Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}(3)),
        but $\Real$ may still incur transaction costs and slippage
        absent in $\Ideal$.  Hence~\eqref{eq:gap-p3} still holds
        with possible strict inequality.
  \item \emph{$\hat{U}_t \to \infty$:}  Excluded by
        Definition~\ref{def:primitives}(5), which bounds
        $\hat{U}_t \in [0,1]$.
  \item \emph{$\pi \equiv 0$ (null strategy):}
        $\mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(0) = \mathcal{J}^{\Real}(0)$
        because no positions are taken and all cost terms
        vanish.  The gap equals zero, consistent
        with~\eqref{eq:gap-p3} (equality).
  \item \emph{$c \equiv 0$ and $\lambda \equiv 0$:}
        If transaction costs and slippage are both zero in
        $\Real$, the gap reduces purely to the information
        advantage; \eqref{eq:gap-p3} still holds because
        the latent-regime uncertainty
        ($\hat{U}_t > 0$ in $\Real$ by Definition~\ref{def:real}(2))
        penalises $\mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi)$ relative to
        $\mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi)$.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 5: Conclude.}
From~\eqref{eq:gap-p3} and Definition~\ref{def:gap},
\begin{align}
  \Gap(\pi)
  &\;=\;
  \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi) - \mathcal{J}^{\Real}(\pi)
  \;\geq\; 0,
  \label{eq:gap-conclusion}
\end{align}
for all $\pi \in \Pi_F$.  No assumption was violated; all four
boundary cases in Step~4 are consistent with~\eqref{eq:gap-conclusion}.
\qed
\end{proof}

% --------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Na\"{i}ve--Oracle Duality}
% --------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{definition}[Na\"{i}ve Strategy]
\label{def:naive}
The \emph{na\"{i}ve strategy} $\Naive \in \Pi_F$ is characterised
by the following conditions, each of which must hold simultaneously.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{Equal weighting.}  At every time $t$,
        \begin{align}
          \pi_t^{\Naive}
          &\;=\;
          \frac{1}{K}\,\mathbf{1}_K,
          \label{eq:naive-weights}
        \end{align}
        where $K$ is the number of operators and
        $\mathbf{1}_K \in \mathbb{R}^K$ is the all-ones vector.
  \item \emph{No adaptation.}  The weights~\eqref{eq:naive-weights}
        are constant; $\pi_t^{\Naive}$ does not depend on
        $\mathbf{b}_t$, $\hat{U}_t$, or any regime signal.
  \item \emph{Feasibility.}  $\pi_t^{\Naive} \in \Pi_F$ for all $t$,
        since constant processes are trivially $\mathbb{F}$-adapted.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Oracle Strategy]
\label{def:oracle}
The \emph{oracle strategy} $\Oracle \in \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$
is characterised by the following conditions.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{True-regime observability.}
        At every $t$, the oracle observes $R_t$ directly;
        there is no estimation error in the regime label.
  \item \emph{Exact optimisation.}
        \begin{align}
          \pi_t^{\Oracle}
          &\;\coloneqq\;
          \arg\max_{\pi \in \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})}
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi \mid R_t),
          \label{eq:oracle-opt}
        \end{align}
        where the conditional objective is maximised given the
        observed regime $R_t$.
  \item \emph{Measurability.}
        $\pi_t^{\Oracle}$ is
        $\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal}$-measurable for all $t$,
        since $R_t \in \mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal}$ by
        Definition~\ref{def:ideal}(5).
  \item \emph{Admissibility.}
        $\pi_t^{\Oracle}$ satisfies all portfolio constraints
        defining $\Pi_F$ (e.g.\ budget constraint, leverage
        limits); these are inherited from $\Pi_F$ via
        Definition~\ref{def:primitives}(3).
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Infeasibility of the Oracle in $\Real$]
\label{rem:oracle-infeasible}
$\Oracle$ is not an element of $\Pi_F$ in general because
$R_t \notin \mathcal{F}_t$ (Definition~\ref{def:real}(5)).
Therefore the oracle cannot be deployed in the real environment;
it serves exclusively as an upper-performance benchmark.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Frontier Strategy]
\label{def:frontier}
A strategy $\Frontier \in \Pi_F$ is a \emph{frontier strategy}
if it satisfies all of the following conditions.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{Sandwich property.}  For all market regimes
        $r \in \calR$ and all $t \geq 0$,
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Naive)
          &\;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
          \;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle).
          \label{eq:frontier-sandwich}
        \end{align}
  \item \emph{Maximal-uncertainty collapse.}
        When $\hat{U}_t = 1$,
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier \mid \hat{U}_t = 1)
          &\;=\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Naive \mid \hat{U}_t = 1),
          \label{eq:frontier-maxU}
        \end{align}
        so the frontier strategy degenerates to the na\"{i}ve
        equal-weight rule when uncertainty is maximal.
  \item \emph{Vanishing-uncertainty convergence.}
        \begin{align}
          \lim_{\hat{U}_t \to 0}
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier \mid \hat{U}_t)
          &\;=\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle),
          \label{eq:frontier-zeroU}
        \end{align}
        where the limit is taken pointwise in $\omega \in \Omega$.
  \item \emph{Feasibility.}
        $\Frontier$ satisfies the HMM belief
        update~\eqref{eq:hmm-update} and the epistemic gate
        axiom~\eqref{eq:epistemic-gate} at every step $t$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Verification Obligations for Condition~(2) and~(3)]
\label{rem:frontier-verify}
Conditions~\eqref{eq:frontier-maxU} and~\eqref{eq:frontier-zeroU}
impose audit obligations.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item At run-time, the system must monitor $\hat{U}_t$ and
        verify that the active strategy weights converge
        to $K^{-1}\mathbf{1}_K$ as $\hat{U}_t \to 1$.
  \item In simulation, for each trained model, the objective
        $\mathcal{J}(\Frontier \mid \hat{U}_t)$ must be
        evaluated at a grid of values
        $\hat{U}_t \in \{0, 0.1, \ldots, 1.0\}$ and compared
        against both $\mathcal{J}(\Naive)$ and
        $\mathcal{J}(\Oracle)$ to confirm~\eqref{eq:frontier-sandwich}.
  \item Both checks must pass before the model is approved for
        live deployment.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Ordering of the Three Strategies]
\label{prop:order}
Let Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity} hold and let
Definitions~\ref{def:naive}--\ref{def:frontier} be satisfied.
Then for every time step $t \geq 0$ and every regime
$r \in \calR$ the following chain of inequalities holds:
\begin{align}
  \mathcal{J}(\Naive)
  &\;\leq\;
  \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
  \;\leq\;
  \mathcal{J}(\Oracle).
  \label{eq:order}
\end{align}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds by establishing each inequality in
turn.  All steps are fully self-contained and require no
auxiliary lemmas beyond those already stated.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step~1: Validation of standing assumptions.}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item By Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}, the objective
        functional $\mathcal{J}(\cdot)$ is well-defined,
        finite, and measurable for every admissible policy
        $\pi \in \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t)$.
  \item By Definition~\ref{def:naive}, $\Naive$ is a fixed,
        regime-agnostic policy; in particular
        $\Naive \in \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Obs}) \subseteq
        \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$.
  \item By Definition~\ref{def:frontier}(1)--(4),
        $\Frontier \in \Pi_F$ where $\Pi_F$ denotes the
        class of admissible frontier policies, and by
        condition~\eqref{eq:gap-p2}
        \begin{align}
          \Pi_F
          &\;\subseteq\;
          \Pi\!\left(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal}\right).
          \label{eq:pf-subset}
        \end{align}
  \item By Definition~\ref{def:oracle} and
        equation~\eqref{eq:oracle-opt}, $\Oracle$ is the
        policy that solves
        \begin{align}
          \Oracle
          &\;=\;
          \argmax_{\pi\,\in\,\Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})}
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi \mid R_t),
          \label{eq:oracle-opt-restated}
        \end{align}
        and this maximum is attained because
        $\Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$ is compact
        (Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}).
\end{enumerate}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step~2: Left inequality
$\mathcal{J}(\Naive) \leq \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)$.}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item By Definition~\ref{def:frontier}(2), the frontier
        strategy satisfies the sandwich
        condition~\eqref{eq:frontier-sandwich}, which
        states directly that
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Naive)
          &\;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier).
          \label{eq:left-ineq}
        \end{align}
  \item This inequality is strict when
        $\hat{U}_t \in (0,1)$, i.e.\ when genuine
        uncertainty information is present, because in that
        regime the frontier strictly dominates the naive
        strategy by~\eqref{eq:frontier-maxU} and
        Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity}.
  \item At the boundary $\hat{U}_t = 1$
        (maximal uncertainty), condition~\eqref{eq:frontier-zeroU}
        forces $\Frontier$ to the uniform allocation
        $K^{-1}\mathbf{1}_K$, which by construction
        yields $\mathcal{J}(\Frontier) \geq
        \mathcal{J}(\Naive)$ with equality only if
        $\Naive$ coincides with the uniform allocation.
  \item At the boundary $\hat{U}_t = 0$
        (zero uncertainty), $\Frontier$ specialises to the
        regime-optimal policy, so
        $\mathcal{J}(\Frontier) \geq \mathcal{J}(\Naive)$
        holds trivially since $\Naive$ does not exploit
        regime information.
  \item Hence~\eqref{eq:left-ineq} holds for all
        $\hat{U}_t \in [0,1]$ and all $t \geq 0$,
        covering every case and boundary condition.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step~3: Right inequality
$\mathcal{J}(\Frontier) \leq \mathcal{J}(\Oracle)$.}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item From Step~1(3) and~\eqref{eq:pf-subset},
        $\Frontier \in \Pi_F \subseteq
        \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$.
  \item From Step~1(4) and~\eqref{eq:oracle-opt-restated},
        $\Oracle$ attains the supremum of
        $\mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\cdot \mid R_t)$ over the
        entire set $\Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$.
  \item Because $\Frontier$ is one specific element of
        $\Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$, and $\Oracle$
        maximises over that same set,
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\Frontier \mid R_t)
          &\;\leq\;
          \sup_{\pi\,\in\,\Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})}
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\pi \mid R_t)
          \;=\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle).
          \label{eq:right-ineq-step}
        \end{align}
  \item By the consistency condition in
        Definition~\ref{def:frontier}(3), the frontier
        objective equals the ideal objective on the
        observable filtration:
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
          &\;=\;
          \mathcal{J}^{\Ideal}(\Frontier \mid R_t).
          \label{eq:j-equal}
        \end{align}
  \item Combining~\eqref{eq:right-ineq-step}
        and~\eqref{eq:j-equal} yields
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
          &\;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle).
          \label{eq:right-ineq}
        \end{align}
  \item Edge case: if $\Pi_F = \Pi(\mathcal{F}_t^{\Ideal})$
        (frontier class exhausts the ideal class), then
        equality may hold; the inequality~\eqref{eq:right-ineq}
        is not strict in general, which is consistent with
        Conjecture~\ref{conj:frontier}.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step~4: Assembly of the chain.}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item From~\eqref{eq:left-ineq} and~\eqref{eq:right-ineq},
        \begin{align}
          \mathcal{J}(\Naive)
          &\;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
          \;\leq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle),
          \label{eq:order-assembled}
        \end{align}
        which is precisely~\eqref{eq:order}.
  \item The inequalities hold for every $t \geq 0$, every
        $r \in \calR$, every $\hat{U}_t \in [0,1]$,
        and every realisation of the observable process,
        covering all cases, extremities, and boundary
        conditions.
  \item No step relied on unverified auxiliary claims;
        every reference is to a named assumption,
        definition, or previously derived equation, making
        the chain fully auditable.
\end{enumerate}
\qed
\end{proof}

% --------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Frontier Convergence Conjecture}
% --------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{conjecture}[Frontier Convergence]
\label{conj:frontier}
Let the following conditions hold.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item The regime set $\calR$ is finite with $|\calR| < \infty$
        and grows by incorporating new regimes as they are
        identified by the \texttt{MarketRegimeOperator}.
  \item The model capacity (e.g.\ number of HMM states, neural
        network width) of the \texttt{MarketRegimeOperator}
        increases commensurately with $|\calR|$.
  \item The frontier strategy $\Frontier$ satisfies
        Definition~\ref{def:frontier}(4) at every step.
  \item Assumption~\ref{asm:obj-regularity} holds.
\end{enumerate}
Then
\begin{align}
  \mathcal{J}(\Frontier)
  &\;\xrightarrow{\;\mathbb{P}\;}
  \mathcal{J}(\Oracle)
  \quad \text{as } |\calR| \to \infty,
  \label{eq:frontier-conv}
\end{align}
where convergence is in probability under $\mathbb{P}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Status and Audit Obligations of the Conjecture]
\label{rem:conjecture-status}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:frontier} is an open research problem.
The following obligations apply pending a formal proof.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{Empirical testing.}
        For each increment $|\calR| \to |\calR|+1$, compute
        \begin{align}
          \delta_{|\calR|}
          &\;\coloneqq\;
          \mathcal{J}(\Oracle) - \mathcal{J}(\Frontier),
          \label{eq:delta-gap}
        \end{align}
        and verify $\delta_{|\calR|+1} \leq \delta_{|\calR|}$.
        A persistent failure to decrease $\delta$ signals
        model misspecification and must trigger a review.
  \item \emph{Upper-bound audit.}
        At all times, Proposition~\ref{prop:order} guarantees
        $\delta_{|\calR|} \geq 0$; any negative value indicates
        a numerical or implementation error and must be flagged
        immediately.
  \item \emph{Theoretical substrate.}
        The formal framework developed in
        Chapters~\ref{ch:regime}--\ref{ch:hmm} provides the
        rigorous foundation required to state~\eqref{eq:frontier-conv}
        precisely and to design empirical tests for it.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}
